    “Good,” Kara hums into her ear. Her warm breath spills over the back of Lena’s neck, one hand coming down to splay wide over her hip. “Now bend your knees, and remember: chicken wings.”

    Lena nods. Right, right. Wait. “What?”

    Kara’s smile is infinitely patient. “You want your elbows up, so the bat comes down at the correct angle.”

    Right. Lena recenters herself, tuning into the moment — the crowd around them, the disjointed carnival jingles occasionally drowning out the dance music — and bounces her arms a little, testing the weight of the baseball bat resting firmly in her grip. “Like this?”

    Kara steps away and eyes her critically. “I think you’ve got it! Now, let me show you how you swing.”

    Lena watches as Kara demonstrates — she’s a pro, of course, endlessly at ease — and lets her thoughts get away from her again. She’s reminded of another night, a hundred years ago or so, when she had watched as Kara intently manhandled a batch of bread dough in Sam and Alex’s kitchen. Lena had been thirsty then, too, and mildly inebriated, equally aware of all the reasons she shouldn’t kiss her and growing similarly unconvinced of their importance.

    Kara turns to her again, casually spinning the bat with the fingers of a single hand. “Are you ready? Or do you want to watch me one more time?”

    Lena sizes up the pitching machine in the back of the cage, still feeling intimidated. “You know,” she says, “I just remembered I have a board meeting first thing Monday morning. And I bruise like a peach.”

    “I remember,” Kara says with a laugh, and then she freezes, apparently not hearing the words until they’d left her mouth. “I mean. Hang on.”

    She returns with a bright red helmet, still looking a little flushed. “So the machine is set to only 40mph, and I can personally guarantee no balls will go anywhere near your face. But if it makes you feel better—”

    And then Kara watches, mesmerized, as Lena watches her, taking her hair down so Kara can help her slip the helmet over her head.

    The outfits Kara has seen her in, Lena swears to god.

    Lena looks up at her from behind the bulky metal faceguard and gives her a wry smile. “I know. Hot, right?”

    Kara wets her lips before she laughs a little, the tips of her ears bright red, but she doesn’t look away. “Smoking.”

    They end up facing off with the pitching machine together, Lena allowing herself to be turned this way and that, wiggling back into Kara’s body a bit as Kara slides her fingers over Lena’s own, wrapping them tight around the bat. Kara guides her movement until Lena strikes, the clean crack of it reverberating up her arm. Kara follows the movement of the ball with her body, taking Lena with her, and she’s a little dizzy, a little giddy, and completely unaware her center of gravity has shifted until she’s steadying herself against Kara’s chest and watching the world tilt off its axis.

    “I got it,” Lena laughs, a little disbelieving. “Did you see that? I actually hit it!”

    “Did I see it? Are you kidding me?” Kara’s beaming pride is contagious, and Lena allows herself to bask in her buoyancy as she clumsily drops the bat and begins to remove her helmet. “That was a line drive to left field!” Kara raves, taking the helmet from her. “The short stop wouldn’t have even had time to get to that!” She reaches out with one hand, still mid-rant, looking like she isn’t even thinking about it as she pushes Lena’s hair out of her face, tucking it behind her ear. “You’re amazing.”

    Lena stills. The tender familiarity of the gesture and the way Kara had done it almost unconsciously stir something inside of her — low, deep, yearning — and she’s moved closer before she’s made a conscious decision to, almost swaying on her feet. Kara’s free hand falls to Lena’s hip again, heavy and warm, as if she’s steadying her still, but instead of holding her in place, she’s tugging her closer.

    In a breathless moment, Lena feels their foreheads bumping, Kara’s nose brushing against her own. Kara is a blazing furnace. Lena just wants to come in from the cold.

    But Kara pulls away abruptly, looking genuinely apologetic. “Shit,” she says. “I’m so sorry. I shouldn’t have done that.”

    Lena is reeling from the sudden shift in mood. “Kara—”

    But Kara doesn't hear it for what it is. “I know,” she says, shaking her head, a deep crease between her brows. “That wasn’t okay. You were so clear about what you want this to be and I keep overstepping. I keep getting it wrong.”

    Before Lena can even attempt to intercept Kara’s spiral, Jess walks up carrying two bottles of Gatorade. “You guys look like you need Jesus,” she says, eyeing them both.

    Lena blinks. Kara does too.

    “He tells me he’s a masseur,” Jess clarifies. “Could clear all this tension I’m sensing here right up. And what’s that about, anyway?” Her eyebrow ticks up meaningfully. “It looked like you were just about to score.”

    “What’s this?” Lena asks, ignoring Jess' loaded remark in favor of the bottles she's brought them.

    “Time to hydrate,” Jess explains as she hands them over. “We can’t win a war on alcohol alone. Seriously though, where were you headed, second base? Third?”

    “You saw that hit?” Lena asks, a foreign, almost childlike sort of pride overruling her annoyance at Jess' jokes. “That was impressive, right? I mean, Kara did most of the work, but—”

    “Mm-hmm.” Jess looks her over with a significant glance. “Good for you.”

    Lena flusters at her assistant’s insinuation. “Stop putting words in my mouth,” she instructs.

    Jess says, “Not even this one?” and promptly deposits a crazy straw spelling out ‘bottom’ into Lena’s Gatorade.

    Lena removes it with a glare before taking a deep drink directly from the bottle, frowning when a spilled drop makes its way down the side. She lifts a thumb to catch it, presses it between her lips. Makes the grave mistake of looking up at Kara as she tries not to think of all the places she could be pressing it, instead.

    Kara has dropped the helmet, apparently without noticing she’s done it, and she’s looking at Lena as if she’s hypnotized, her gaze locked firmly on Lena’s mouth. Her other hand is clenched so tight around her own bottle that Lena fears for its survival. She only blinks back into awareness after long moments pass, cheeks turning pink as she catches the knowing look on Jess’ face. She wobbles a little, and begins to slowly back away. “I should just... go over there,” she tells them. “To the... thing.”

    Jess suggests, helpfully, “The bathrooms?”

    Kara picks up speed, still fully walking backwards. “That,” she says. “Those.” She takes off at a near run.

    Jess nudges Lena. “What are you still doing here?”

    Lena frowns. “I’m rehydrating.”

    “You’re procrastinating,” Jess says, slowly as if addressing a stubborn child. “She’s clearly waiting for you to come after her.”

    Lena snorts. “No she’s not.”

    Jess rolls her eyes and huffs. “Suit yourself then, Princess,” she says. “I’m going to go find Jesus.”

    -

    Jess is a liar. When Lena enters the restroom, it’s immediately obvious Kara wasn’t expecting her at all. She’s leaned forward over the sink, staring into the mirror, looking furious at herself. When their eyes meet in the mirror, she jump-turns to face her, pulling her hands behind her back as if she’s been caught doing something shameful.

    Except she’s standing in front of a mirror, so Lena can see — well, practically all of her. Like the little golden flyaway hairs that have tugged loose from Kara’s bun, glinting in the fluorescent light. The ripple of her abs under her tight cotton top, as well as the way the fabric gathers at the curve of her spine. The deep crease of consternation between her brows, and her fingers, clutching each other in a grip so tight her knuckles have turned a stark white.

    “Lena,” she says. She’s still not blinking. “Are we—” she untangles her hands, moving them to grip the counter behind her instead. Her Gatorade bottle sits beside them, now mostly empty save for a crazy straw looping out the word ‘stud’.

    Fucking Jess.

    Kara swallows. “Is everything okay?”

    Lena takes a few tentative steps forward, letting the door fall closed behind her. “Don’t ask me,” she says cautiously. “I’m not the one who ran off.”

    Kara snorts a little, strategically averting her eyes. “I didn’t run,” she lies. “I just, you know. Needed—” she releases one hand to wave awkwardly at the stalls.

    “Oh.” Lena falters. Use your words, aunt Lena, she remembers. “It’s just that, back there in the batting cage,” she pushes through, “I thought for a moment that we both wanted… but then, when you stopped me—”

    “Wanted…?” Kara says faintly. Then her eyes flick back up at Lena’s as if she’s just grown a second head, and Lena knows L-Corp is nowhere near ready to take that step in human reproductive cloning. “Stopped you?” Kara reels.

    The force of Kara’s surprise almost knocks Lena over. Oh, she thinks. This was a bad idea. This was a tequila-fueled idea. Also entirely Jess’ fault, rewarding her employer’s generosity and apparent gullibility by luring her into this trap. “Nevermind,” Lena orders succinctly, turning on her heel.

    But Kara halts her retreat, her fingers catching Lena’s wrist, tugging gently until Lena looks at her again. When she does, the forwardness of the wanting in Kara’s gaze hits Lena like a fist. “Wait,” she says. “You wanted me to kiss you?”

    Lena takes a shallow breath and contemplates her answer.

    No would be an obvious lie.

    Yes, an understatement.

    “I never stopped wanting to kiss you,” she blurts. Something wound tight within her tugs loose with the confession.

    Kara pushes off the sink, darting closer before stopping herself. Her gaze flicks back and forth between Lena’s eyes a couple of times, as if she’s searching for something, before drifting inevitably back down to Lena’s lips. Lena resists the urge to wet them with her tongue, to show off the soft pink heat that could be Kara’s, if she still wants it the way Lena wants her.

    Kara swallows again. Lena watches the movement of Kara’s throat as she waits.

    Two beats pass.

    Kara’s hands are on her before Lena ever sees her move. Confident fingers brush against the sensitive skin of her nape, familiar and intent, and it’s like a match being struck — fire races down Lena’s back, burning low in the tip of her spine, heat blooming low in her belly, and in an instant Lena forgets everything she’s ever learned about using her legs to remain standing. Kara catches her smoothly, pulling her in until their hips connect and spinning them around until Lena is half-sitting, half-pinned against the counter. It helps with the standing part, a little, but it also creates this whole other problem where Lena now has trouble remembering how to breathe.

    “Kara,” she says, trying — failing — not to tremble. “Please.”

    Kara’s eyelids droop a little at the final word, her gaze dropping down to where Lena knows her dress has been pulled aside to reveal her garters, Kara’s fingers twitching at her waist.

    And then she throws Lena into one of the toilet stalls, slamming the door in her face.

    “Alex!” Lena hears Kara sing out cheerfully on the other side. “What brings you here?”

    There’s a pause, during which Lena inexpertly maneuvers her body onto the toilet seat until she’s certain her feet wouldn't be spotted if someone was looking under the door. And then Alex hisses, “Kara, what do you think you’re doing?”

    One day Lena is going to murder this woman.

    “Um,” Kara says, sounding like she’s not entirely sure herself. “Peeing, I guess? What, um. What did you think I was doing?”

    “Don’t play dumb with me,” Alex says. “I’m talking about what’s been happening out there.” Lena flinches at the scowl in Alex’s voice when she alleges, “You’ve been flirting with her all night.”

    Kara panics audibly, almost backing into the stall where Lena is hiding. “I’m n— Wait. What?” She fumbles, and then, disastrously, “Lena isn’t—”

    Fortunately, Alex’s anger travels quicker than Kara’s inability to lie. “Kara,” she says dangerously, “What did we agree on, the last time we talked about this?”

    It’s quiet for a moment. Lena thinks she sees Kara’s shadow shift her weight from foot to foot. She says, in a small voice, “I’d really prefer not to get into this right now.”

    But Alex isn’t having it. “I need to hear you say it, Kara.”

    Kara mumbles, after a sigh, in a monotone so soft Lena almost misses it, “We agreed I’m going to respect Lena’s—”

    “— Lena’s boundaries,” Alex says with her, to Lena’s shock and consternation. “That's right. And what does that not look like?”

    Kara is whispering now, and so flustered that Lena is sure Alex will catch on to Lena’s perplexed presence any second. “Look,” she hushes, “managing my expectations is not as easy as you made it sound when she’s—” she stops herself, squirming. “Also Sam isn’t exactly helping me maintain a respectful distance. Have you considered maybe your fiancée is the one in need of a lesson about boundaries?”

    Hear hear, Lena thinks. She’s impressed, and also her left foot is now skidding slowly, inevitably, off the side of the toilet seat.

    Alex’s voice is kinder when she speaks up again, but her message is no less pointed. “She told you ‘no’, Kara,” she says. “She’s trying to be your friend.” There’s a pause, during which neither sister speaks and Lena hangs on for dear life to the pipe above the tank. And then Alex concludes, devastatingly, “She’s my friend, too. I don’t want to see either of you get hurt and go radio silent again.”

    Well damn.

    “That’s— actually really thoughtful, Alex,” Kara agrees quietly. “I love you too, you know.”

    “Yeah yeah, whatever,” Alex snaps. “Go wash your hands and get out here. Final round is in ten minutes and Jess says it’s a team against team deathmatch. We need to discuss strategy. Where is Lena, anyway?”

    “I have absolutely no idea,” Kara says as they exit the restroom together.

    Lena’s foot slips to the floor the moment the door slams shut behind them, but she hardly notices anymore. She sits on the seat, reeling, feeling flustered, and wet, and a little dazed, and also decidedly, inexplicably, unjustifiably kissed.

    Right Within Your Heart

    Chapter Notes

    This is a long one, just under twelve thousand words, so, you know. Settle in. Maybe grab some coffee. Maybe make it Irish; I’m not changing the rating to E, but this chapter made me seriously consider it. Because of all the blasphemy. And the— other thing.

    new mood board for this chapter & more brilliant illustrations by battenthecrosshatches

    It’s something, Lena thinks, taking on what feels like the entirety of National City’s late-night, mild-to-highly intoxicated population with a fondness for violent gunplay, as well as her own near-insurmountable desire to drop out of the game and onto her knees before the world’s goofiest Greek goddess.

    It’s something, she thinks, and that something is agony.

    To the deep detriment of Lena’s sanity, Jess has their team spread out over the dimly lit basement for the final round. Lena is granted only the occasional glimpse of Kara, brief flashes of glory and golden grace. Lena watches her — when she isn’t scrambling for cover or firing her phaser — much in the way she imagines the Aechaeans had once watched Achilles on the battlefields of Troy.

    They’ve cruelly been denied an opportunity to talk, or, more optimistically, to find themselves a dark corner where Lena can drag Kara’s hand up her dress and press her fingers between her legs. Their communication is limited entirely to their team’s open channel, where every single one of their friends would be privy to each incriminating word exchanged between them.

    “Yo Princess,” Jess’ voice crackles through the device strapped to Lena’s shoulder, “What’s taking so long? Did you fall asleep back there?”

    Lena rolls her eyes before venturing briefly out of cover. She’s meant to be tagging flags for the blue squad while the others focus on taking out the four opposing teams, but so far it’s proven to be a somewhat Sysiphian task. “I’m up against your buddy Jesus,” she gripes. “He’s taking them back the moment the one-minute lockout expires.” She growls when she spots him, peeking out over a half wall just a couple of feet away — then yelps and takes a frantic dive when she realizes his next shot is aimed directly at her.

    “I’m disappointed in you, Bruiser,” Sam’s voice cuts in. “I thought you were going to bring him around to our side.”

    “Hell no,” Jess sneers, her breath coming out in short huffs. “Guy’s a total narcissist. Completely abandoned me in the arena last round and then, just before we went in for this one, had the nerve to claim he’d been carrying me.” There’s a short pause, filled by the faint but unmistakable sounds of an enemy player being eliminated. “Fuck him.”

    Lena pokes out of cover again, careful to keep her vest shielded this time as she neatly lines up her shot. She smiles, pleased with herself, when the flag lights up blue on her very first try.

    And then she realizes the lamb of god is nowhere to be seen, and two more previously blue flags have turned red.

    “Son of a bitch,” she seethes. “Why am I doing this, again? For a tacky plastic trophy boasting our embarrassing team name and a free bucket of buffalo wings?” She plots out a course for better shelter and bolts, tagging another flag along the way. “I can buy every one of you a hundred of those things,” she pants, “without risking my mental health and professional reputation.”

    “It’s a bottomless bucket of wings,” Kara answers her, her voice tinted with a sort of ardent devotion one would usually reserve for an unexplained, perhaps magical phenomenon. “I’ll come and give you a hand,” she offers. “Just stay where you are.”

    Yes, please. Lena is already tugging at her dress and smoothing back her hair, ready to fall into her hero’s arms once more. But Jess throws a swift wrench in her plans. “Negative, Supergirl,” she decides sternly. “We need you in your sector. Commander, do you read?”

    Lena’s shoulders sag a little, though she knows it’s probably for the best — she’s at least 75% sure Sara mentioned a rule against physical contact in the arena.

    “Loud and clear,” Alex answers. “Princess, what’s your twenty?”

    Lena blinks. “Come again?”

    Alex heaves out an impatient sigh. “Where are you?”

    “Um.” Lena squints at her surroundings, feeling foolish. “At the corner of… blinky pink and that large, lime green something-or-other?”

    “She’s in sector four, west corner, near the far wall,” Jess clips in. “About fifteen feet from your position.”

    Lena promptly shoots Alex when she arrives. She doesn't feel particularly bad about it, either — she’s actually really enjoying this new dynamic where she gets to open fire on Alex every time she startles her.

    “Watch it!” Alex hisses. “Friendly fire!”

    Lena shrugs. “Consider it payback for years of sneaking up on me like that.”

    “Whatever,” Alex grumbles, acknowledging Lena’s improved form with reluctant respect as she joins her behind cover. “At least you’re actually hitting your targets, now.”

    Lena should hope so. She has, after all, at this point spent approximately nine hundred years in this basement practicing. “All but the one that truly matters,” she says gravely. “Are you going to help me take him down?”

    Alex grins. “With pleasure.”

    Sadly, the prince of peace seems to have gone into hiding upon Alex’s arrival. “Clever,” Alex remarks. “Stealth is half the game — you should never let your opponents know where you are.”

    They both jump when someone starts shouting just to their left, accompanied by the hateful wails of phaser fire. “Hey asshole! Over here!” A familiar voice calls out. “Yeah, that’s right! You really thought you’d get a piece of this, huh? Go on, run home to your mama!”

    It’s Sam, of course. She lands behind them with a thud, her vest and phaser still shrieking and flashing in obvious distress.

    Alex blinks down at her. “Though evidently that tactic is a matter of personal preference.”

    “Hey babes!” Sam smiles up at them from where she’s sprawled out on the floor. “Have we won yet? The suspense is killing me. You know, in addition to the unrelenting gunfire.”

    “Seriously,” Alex sighs, grabbing her fiancée's arm to help her to her feet. “How are you still alive?”

    Sam huffs. “I do not know. I’ve got—” She checks her phaser, grunts. “Fifteen HP left.”

    “And of course you’ve brought half the red team down upon us,” Alex says, taking a quick glance around the corner. “Damn it!”

    Sam nudges her shoulder with her own. “It’s okay. Always figured I’d go out looking young and pretty, and in the most badass way possible. What could be better than sacrificing myself for the greatest good?”

    Alex may tear up a little. It’s such a rare sight that Lena can’t be sure.

    “Listen,” Sam says. “You can make a break for it. I'll give you cover fire and take that slippery bastard out for good.”

    “I’m with you,” Alex promises solemnly. “Until the very end.”

    The brides-to-be move in perfect sync, exchanging one last, loaded look before throwing themselves recklessly into the line of fire. As Lena bumbles across the battlefield between them, one arm pressed close across her chest for support, she finds a moment to envy their auspicious choice of attire. Judging by their surprise when they arrived, Sam and Alex were just as unprepared for the evening’s activities as Lena had been, and yet—

    Lena slows, absently putting down an advancing enemy with a single, distracted shot.

    Funny, she thinks, how Alex just so happens to be perfectly styled for this classy carnage, her chic-yet-comfortable black romper complemented by a pair of thick-soled suede high tops. How especially hilarious, the way Samantha Luciana Gabriela Arias, Lena’s BFF and the world’s most abominable brat, is — sure — wearing her dangliest silver hoops above her sequined cocktail dress, but the seam running down the back of her stockings disappears into a perfectly practical pair of running shoes.

    The words “You fuckers” have barely made it out of Lena’s mouth when Sam’s vest lights up a dramatic red before going entirely dark, her phaser blasting out a final, mournful sound. Alex catches her around the waist as if to keep her from keeling over, caught up in the theatrics of it all.

    “Yep,” Sam says, utterly nonchalant, glancing down at her display. “Guess I’m a goner, huh?”

    Alex clenches her jaw so hard Lena can see the muscle moving. “So is Jesus,” she promises, grim. “He just doesn’t know it yet.”

    Sam grins at her. “Aw, babe,” she says, endeared, “are you going to avenge me? Have so much fun with that.” She touches a thumb to Alex’s cheek before kissing her, raising her hand to her temple in a final salute before turning on her heel. “If you want me, I’ll be in the bar.”

    “Ten-four,” Alex says, and then Lena is alone again — Sam ascending to a better place, Alex storming forth, off on her own heroic character arc.

    “So, Ms. Luthor,” Jess comes in, sounding a little unsure, and not just because of the tinny connection. “If I asked you very nicely not to panic—”

    Lena panics, instantly. “Spit it out, Jessica.”

    On the other end of the line, her assistant audibly gulps. “It’s your new friend Eve,” she says. “She just tagged your official account on Twitter. There are pictures.”

    Of course she did. Of course there are.

    Well, Lena thinks, that’s fun. She’s looking forward to seeing what her mother will have to say about it. The pictures can’t be flattering — it’s not like they had stopped for selfies after Alex had so helpfully wrestled Ms. Teschmacher to the ground. They likely show Lena cowering in a corner, much like she is right now, looking like she’s hiding, miserable, morose. The thought rubs at her, chafing at her pride.

    She scowls.

    Lena Luthor doesn’t cower. Doesn’t capitulate. Lena Luthor studies her competition, cool and calculating, and then she kills it.

    Her friends’ voices fade out, after that. It’s possible Lena just doesn’t hear them anymore. She’s on a mission of her own now, ready to do some actual damage, to set the record straight.

    -

    It turns out to be a bit trickier than she expects. With less than ten minutes left in the game, the crowd has thinned out a lot — likely having fallen by the wayside of Alex’s warpath. The only real resistance Lena encounters comes from a terrifyingly tiny brunette who moves like quicksilver, dancing out of the way of Lena’s shots.

    Once Lena deals with her, every flag she tags for their team stays blue.

    “Nice work!”

    Lena’s fingers twitch against her weapon as she whirls around, trying to find the origin of the sound. It was Kara’s voice, unmistakably, unmangled by radio static.

    Lena finally spots her high overhead, swinging feet-up from the rafters like a radioactive bat.

    “Lucky shot,” Lena deflects, frowning. “How on earth did you get up there?”

    Kara answers her with a grin and an upside-down shrug, silky flyaway hairs fanning out around her head in a perfect approximation of a halo. “I thought I was the last one left,” she says. “I’m surprised you’re still standing.”

    Lena can’t hold it against her; she’s pretty shocked herself. “Through no fault of my own, I’m sure.”

    They regard each other for a long, electric moment. Lena notes that there’s no trace of the nervous, almost jittery energy that Kara had given off in the restroom earlier; in its place is a pleased, almost insolent sort of self-confidence. The last time they were alone, Lena had been desperate to have Kara’s hands on her again. And Kara clearly knows it.

    Lena fears she’s savoring it.

    “I can see your work from up here,” Kara says. “You’ve left a trail of blue flags in your wake. It’s impressive.”

    Lena bites the inside of her cheek to keep from saying something stupid, conscious of the radios strapped to their chests, transmitting every word they say directly to whatever teammates they have left.

    “You haven’t seen the others?” Lena fishes. “Haven’t… heard, from anyone?”

    “No,” Kara says, glancing down at her vest. “My radio went dead a while ago. I know Jess is out.”

    “Sam is too,” Lena says. “I’m not sure about your sister. She’s been quiet for some time, but— she does that.”

    Kara huffs out a laugh as she nimbly extricates herself from the rafters. “She’s probably just in stealth mode.” She lands on her feet beside Lena, solid but weightless, like a broad-shouldered bouncy ball. Lena sways a little at her unexpected nearness before realizing Kara is only leaning in so she can use her radio. “Alex?” she tries, and then, after a deep breath, rolling her eyes at Lena dramatically, “Come in, Commander?”

    Lena is mesmerized by the bright blue intensity of Kara’s eyes. Something feels different, in the air between them, the reality of what’s been confessed laid bare like a live wire, dangerously exposed.

    There’s no answer from Alex, not even a crackle of static on the line.

    “Do you think,” Kara muses, her breath glancing off Lena’s cheek, “it might just be the two of us now?”

    Lena gnaws at her bottom lip. “If that’s the case,” she mutters with enormous effort, “may god have mercy on us all.”

    Kara laughs. “Aw, baby, no,” she coos, letting the endearment slip with not a single thought to the perilous condition of Lena’s immortal soul. “Don’t tell me you forget,” she says, smoothly and with uncalled-for emphasis, “how good we are, together.”

    Lena stares at her, her heart banging against the bars of her ribcage.

    She swears even the freckles around Kara’s nose are taunting her.

    “Stop it,” she says, far too tender and a little wild, her hands finding the front strap of Kara’s suspenders, tucked just behind her phosphorescent vest. She folds her fingers around the warm leather and tugs, hard. “Just shut up and—”

    “Come in, Supergirl!” An urgent voice crackles in through Lena’s radio. “Princess? Can you hear me?”

    Kara halts, eyes widening in shock, her lips a delicate inch away from Lena’s own. “Jess?” she says with furious incredulity. “I watched you get killed!”

    “I am, unfortunately, very much dead,” Jess confirms, “and so are the Commander and Handsome Sam, both of whom I have here with me.”

    Sam’s voice is all cheer. “Hi guys!”

    “Hi,” Lena whispers wistfully, gazing up into Kara’s eyes before blinking down at her mouth, so close and yet completely out of reach.

    “However,” Jess continues, “There is a very shiny bright side. My radio didn’t switch off when I was eliminated. Some sort of malfunction, I guess.”

    “They cut mine instead,” Kara realizes. “I’ve been unable to communicate from the moment you went down.”

    There’s a sly smile in Jess’ voice. “Which is how we can still win this thing.”

    Jess lays it out succinctly. According to the livestream and stat screens in the abandoned briefing room, only the red and green teams still have players left in the game. “They no longer consider us a threat,” Jess explains, “and they’re so focused on shooting each other that they’ve completely stopped bothering with the flags. There’s only twenty five of them in the arena and, thanks to Lena, we currently hold six.”

    “Just a little less than half,” Sam suggests, and Lena sends up a quick prayer for L-Corp’s financial future, seeing how Sam is her most trusted numbers man.

    “A little less than half of what’s required to win,” Jess clarifies. “We need just seven more. If you capture them close enough to the end of the round — without getting slaughtered, obviously — we win the game.”

    “They’ll never see it coming,” Alex relishes. “Not until it’s already too late.”

    “We have a chance.” Kara sounds awed, staring at Lena a little starry-eyed, no doubt thinking about those buffalo wings.

    Lena is a smidge more skeptical. “So you’re asking us to double our flags, somehow — while in the middle of an active combat zone — and you expect us to take them all within the final sixty seconds?”

    “You could take the first two a little earlier than that,” Jess allows. “They’re far enough away from the enemies’ positions. But you have to save the other five for the final minute, yeah. We can’t risk the other teams stealing them back as soon as their sixty second lockout expires.”

    Kara puts both of her warm palms on Lena’s shoulders. “It’s us,” she says, squeezing gently. “You and me. We can do this.”

    Lena blinks back at her dumbly as her whole entire being, body, soul, everything, simply dilates.

    “Maybe,” Sam says. “I mean, Lena has plenty of ammo and HP left, but Kara, you’re running low on both.”

    “Oh shit,” Alex mutters, ominously. “Lena has to be the tank.”

    “Affirmative,” Jess agrees. “Kara will be the one doing the tagging, but Lena, you have to defend her at any cost.”

    Lena studies Kara’s hopeful expression, focusing on her comforting grip on Lena’s arms. “God,” she says. “Okay.”

    -

    As the timer ticks down to the last five minutes, Jess steers them expertly toward the stage of their final battle. She was right — Red and Green are so wrapped up in each other they barely notice their approach. Things heat up a little as they inch closer to their targets, but they’re okay — Lena gets hit a couple of times, drawing fire away from Kara, but she does her best not to let it bother her. It’s kind of thrilling, actually, no longer having to worry about herself, focusing all of her attention on Kara’s safety, instead.

    They capture the first two flags easily enough, with Jess’ permission and three minutes left on the clock. When they fall into cover, one last time before the final push, they’re both breathing hard, but grinning.

    They make a good team, Lena thinks.

    “This is it, soldiers,” Jess says. “Ninety seconds to the final countdown. Five more flags, and the game is ours.”

    Alex says, solemnly, sounding a little hoarse, “Good luck, Supergirl. And Princess Fussy Pants — it's been an honor fighting at your side.”

    Kara bumps her shoulder into Lena’s own. “Ready?”

    Lena blows out a fortifying breath. “You’d better not be recording this,” she warns Jess.

    Her assistant laughs. “You couldn’t stop me if you tried.”

    Lena blinks back at Kara, admiring the lines around her eyes, the soft downy hair at her temple. She asks, “Any last words of advice?”

    “Yeah,” Kara says, fixing Lena with a blazing intensity. “Don’t get killed.”

    And then, of course—

    Kara gets killed.

    Lena watches it happen as if in slow motion. It’s the good shepherd himself, arising out of nowhere, taking them all completely by surprise. Lena finishes him with a shout, exacting the most furious vengeance she can muster — which is dreadfully anticlimactic, due to the undignified pew! pew! of it all — before throwing herself down onto Kara’s prone, lightless form. “Don’t you die on me!” she orders as Kara grunts beneath her. She claws at Kara’s vest as if she believes static electricity might spark it back to life.

    “Aw.” Kara winks at her. “I didn’t think you cared.”

    “Not to interrupt, guys,” Jess interrupts, “but we’re fast approaching the final minute here.”

    “Let’s go,” Sam says firmly. “It's time to move your ass.”

    Lena sniffles. “Fuck off,” she tells them. “We’re done.”

    Kara grabs her by the front of her vest, frantic. “Lena, no. You can still save us.”

    Lena stares, shocked, at the absolute conviction written on Kara’s face, and clenches her jaw. “How long do we have?”

    “Thirty-eight seconds to countdown,” Jess says, “and we’re now seeing multiple hostiles closing in on your location.”

    “It’s not possible,” Lena argues. “I can’t do it on my own. If I get shot, even once—”

    “We ran into battle for you,” Alex says solemnly. “We don’t expect a victory. We only ask that you honor our sacrifice.”

    “Come on, Luthor,” Sam prods. “Do you want Twitter to call you a quitter? Or are you going to go out a hero, guns blazing?”

    Kara is still holding her gaze, but she’s released her grip on Lena’s vest, her fingers now pressed lightly against the material. As Lena hesitates, Kara’s solemn expression quietly blooms into a confident grin. “No,” she says. “Lena’s going to do more than that. She’s going to get us those bottomless wings.”

    Their friends respond with affectionate skepticism. Lena would laugh along with them, if she wasn’t so utterly captivated by the fire smoldering in Kara’s eyes.

    Kara moves her hand a little higher on Lena’s vest, where the polyester meets her collarbone, and strokes a single fingertip along her skin. “Just imagine how hungry I am,” she goads her, before digging her teeth into her own plush bottom lip. She sends a heated, lingering gaze down Lena’s throat before capturing her eyes again. “Think how grateful I will be.”

    Lena licks her lips. “Fuck losing with honor,” she rasps. “We’re going to win.”

    -

    It takes Lena only a single second to regret her libido-driven lunacy.

    Kara bites the inside of her cheek, her eyes fixed on the road. “But we’re only half an hour away,” she says. “If we keep going, we might still make it in time for dessert.”

    Lena almost feels bad for denying her that. She reaches over, running careful fingers over Kara’s jean-clad thigh in an effort to at least slightly sweeten the deal. “There’s something I have to tell you,” she says, her voice as soft as her heart, “and I don’t want you driving us off the road in an attempt to keep me from saying it.”

    Kara seems to deflate, her cheeks flushing like a perfect peach. She takes a breath, checks the time, their gas meter, the rear view mirror, and then — finally — she pulls over.

    And then she blurts, “My novel is getting published.”

    Fleetwood Mac plays on in the background, filling the car with the simple truth still unspoken between them, while Lena struggles to catch up to Kara’s conversational curveball. She says, very kind and extremely understanding, “What the fuck, Kara?”

    “Yeah,” Kara says. “I have an agent and a contract and everything.” She keeps looking at her, her expression caught somewhere between caution and awe, as if she’s afraid Lena won’t believe her, as if she can’t quite believe it herself. “I’m sorry—”

    Lena wants to shake her, she’s so proud. “You’re sorry?” she asks, laughing again. “You’re getting published! Oh, my god. That’s—” And then she stills, wondering. “How long have you known?”

    Kara looks a little guilty. “They signed me the week after the party,” she says. “I wanted to tell you. I wanted to, right away.”

    Lena tries not to feel wounded. “You dodged every opportunity we had to talk.”

    Kara nods, twisting her fingers in her lap. “I know. I wanted—” she grimaces at herself. “It’s dumb when I say it out loud.”

    Whatever it is, Lena thinks, it can’t be worse than what’s about to come out of her own mouth. Maybe she’ll take a page out of Kara’s book, and just start singing along with Christine McVie.

    “I know what you’re about to tell me,” Kara says. “I just didn’t want you to say it yet.”

    Lena is almost indignant. “That can’t possibly be true,” she says. “I’ve barely figured out how to say it, myself.”

    There’s a thread of defiance in Kara’s voice when she says, “I wanted to see your face when I told you. I wanted you to look at me— the way you look at me when it’s just the two of us. Before...” She huffs, frustrated.

    Lena feels like her chest will burst wide open if Kara says so much as another word. Darling, she thinks, but it comes out sounding a little bit more like, “You’re right. That’s the dumbest thing I’ve ever heard.”

    Kara flashes her a deeply disgruntled look. “Dimples,” she says, as if she’s calling out an illegal move in a game of chess.

    Lena blinks. “What—”

    “You get them,” Kara waves her fingers at her own face, furrowing her brow. “When you smile. When you seem, I don’t know. Genuinely happy. And sometimes it feels like—” There’s a heavy pause. Kara is flushed a deep red now. She swallows, looking uncharacteristically nervous. “Sometimes when you look at me, I feel like—”

    “You make me happy.”

    It takes Lena a second to catch up to the fact that she’s actually said it.

    Fucking finally.

    Kara blinks at her, her eyes flitting between Lena’s own, shiny with sudden tears. Lena feels like there’s still something she’s missing, still something she doesn’t understand. She wants to crawl into Kara’s chest, squeeze her whole body around her big, beautiful heart.

    “Yeah.” Kara breathes it out, softly. “It feels like that. And I know that isn’t— I could tell that you realized it, that morning, before I left. That you knew it wasn’t going to work for you like that. And I mean I get it, why would you want a friends-with-benefits situation with someone who won’t even let you— return the favor, or whatever?”

    And, oh, no.

    Oh god.

    Lena Luthor is a monster.

    Lena’s head spins with the reality of exactly how thoroughly she’s turned Kara around, how confused she’s allowed her to become.

    “I’m so sorry,” she says, squeezing Kara’s thigh, bringing her other hand over for good measure, “But that’s not at all—”

    Kara closes her eyes, shaking her head decisively, balling her hands into fists. “No," she says, "I’m the one who should be apologizing. I was stalling you on purpose. I thought maybe if I could stop you from ending it, then at least this weekend I could still have you.” She falters, taking in a quick breath, her bottom lip trembling. “Even if you don’t— even if I don’t get to keep you.”

    The feeling rises inside Lena again, even stronger this time, overwhelming and enormous, like a wave, or an avalanche. “Darling,” she says, almost lightheaded with the relief of finally letting it spill out, “you make me happy.” She grabs Kara’s fists, pulls them into her belly, anything to have her closer. “You have from the start. All this time, I— I haven't been fair to you at all.”

    Kara is just looking at her, uncomprehending, tearful, her fingers now clutching Lena’s own.

    “I’ve been acting like an idiot,” Lena admits, angry at herself. “I’ve treated you terribly, and I’m so terribly, terribly sorry.”

    Kara’s eyebrows tick up almost imperceptibly. She still looks like she doesn’t quite trust what she’s hearing. “So, that morning—”

    Lena is crying now, too. “God,” she says, lifting Kara’s hands to her face, pressing her lips to her sugar-dusted knuckles. “I wanted you, so much. I want all of you.”

    One of Kara’s hands twitches out of her hold, but instead of moving it away, like Lena fears, Kara uses the pad of her thumb to gently trace the outline of Lena’s bottom lip. Lena shivers. “Why didn’t you just tell me?” Kara rasps.

    “I was afraid,” Lena concedes, surprised by how easy it is to say it, after hiding it all this time. “I always mess these things up. And Sam, her family— they’re all I have. I didn’t want to jeopardize that just because I’m—”

    Yours.

    “—so inexorably drawn to you.”

    Kara’s eyebrow crease is back in full force. “I don’t know what that means.”

    Lena giggles wetly, pressing a kiss into Kara’s palm. “You’re an English teacher.”

    “Soon-to-be published writer, actually,” Kara quips. “But why would us being together jeopardize your bond with Sam? Sam loves me. And she seems pretty fond of you as well.”

    “Okay, so,” Lena says, “Please know that I am in therapy, and I’m not using this as an excuse for my atrocious behavior towards you. But I did warn you that relationships aren’t my forte.”

    Kara is looking at her, still tear-streaked but seeming sturdier by the moment, stroking her thumb over Lena’s cheek. “Maybe you just need practice,” she suggests. “You didn’t seem all that capable of handling yourself in a laser tag arena at first, either.”

    “Would you mind if—” Lena pauses, checking herself. “I don’t want you to think I intend to date you in secret,” she stresses, “Or that I’m keeping my exits clear. But do you think we can keep it between us for just a little while longer? You know how Sam can be.”

    Kara’s smile is easy, her posture loose. “That’s fine,” she says. “This should be her and Alex’s weekend, anyway. We can take things slow, right? One step at a time.”

    Lena is in her lap a heartbeat later, not entirely sure how she made it there, and she’s kissing her, deep and messy and laughing again, their mixed tears salty on their tongues.

    When Kara finally pulls back onto the road, a song or two later, she places a warm hand just above Lena’s knee and squeezes. “I did bargain Waldo for it,” she winks. “So it’s technically mine.”

    And it is, Lena thinks. All of it.

    Hers.

    -

    Evening is inching into night by the time they reach the resort. Lena is deeply relieved to discover no aerial tram ride is required to get them there. The main building lights up against the star-speckled sky like a beacon of warmth and Lena understands instantly why Alex and Sam chose this venue; it looks like an oversized version of their cabin, exposed wooden beams and gigantic windows and a modern-yet-welcoming vibe.

    The lobby is hot when they enter, a fire crackling in the hearth just past the front desk. Jess is waiting for them there. “You missed dinner,” she informs them. “Also, did you happen to bring a gun? Ms. Arias’ mother needs to be shot.”

    Lena blinks at her. “Patricia?” she asks warily. “I know she can get a little—”

    Jess holds up a hand and counts on her fingers, “Delusional? Entitled? Self-obsessed? That woman is completely detached from reality.” She waves them toward the restaurant. “Leave your luggage. We’ll have to get all of that sorted later.”

    The restaurant is empty save for a couple of after-dinner stragglers, and the lights have been dimmed, but it’s clearly ready for a fairytale winter wedding. Lena takes it all in, a little surprised. “The place looks—”

    “Like a Pinterest explosion, I know. Wait until you see the flowers.” Jess rolls her eyes mildly before leading them out to the terrace, where a couple of comfortable-looking daybeds curve around a blazing fire pit. They’re covered in pillows and quilts in the colors Lena recognizes from the wedding invitation, navy and burgundy, with subtle accents of white and gold. An ocean of fairy lights surrounds them.

    “I thought Sam said they were keeping it small,” Lena says, tugging a quilt over her shoulders before joining Jess by the fire. Kara skips the daybeds, wandering over to the edge of the terrace to admire the moonlit view of the mountains beyond.

    Jess looks up at Lena, propping her feet up on the broad stone rim of the fire pit. “Consider what ‘keeping it small’ might mean to one of National City’s wealthiest women who is also madly in love.”

    Lena blushes. She can’t help it, with the way Jess’ words hit right at the heart of her, the way her assistant may as well have just described Lena herself. Lena’s eyes lift to find Kara, still standing across the deck, and she startles when their eyes meet. Kara’s gaze turns questioning, and then warm, and then molten. Her lips twitch, fingers twisting around the railing behind her. Lena breaks into a light sweat at the sight of them, remembering exactly what those hands are capable of.

    Lena returns Kara’s smile, her own fingers curling in her lap, something inside her feeling like it’s unfurling, a muscle stretching after a long period of disuse.

    “Oh my god,” Jess says. “Holy shit.”

    Lena jumps at the sound, tearing her eyes away from Kara, but it’s too late. Jess is half out of her seat, her delighted gaze bouncing back and forth between Kara and herself like she’s following a tennis match. “Did you two finally—”

    “Fuck,” Alex’s voice sounds from behind them, hushed with breathless awe. “Lena, I knew I could count on you to bring the good stuff.” Her eyes are intent on the label of one of the bottles of Glenfiddich Lena had left at the front desk.

    Lena gives her assistant a warning look. Jess smirks at her for a moment but keeps her mouth shut, nimbly excusing herself to find them some glasses.

    “Your timing is perfect,” Alex announces, already deftly unwinding the tin foil covering the cork. “You’re too late to have to suffer through dinner with former Broadway sensation Patty Arias, and just in time for happy hour.”

    Lena forces herself to stop looking at Kara, taking a moment to bask in the heat rolling off the fire. Alex hugs her sister and then tugs her down onto a daybed, covering their legs with a quilt. Kara leans into her, looking handsome and softer than Lena remembers ever seeing her before. Both sisters gleam in the firelight, their auburn and golden hair aflame.

    Lena smiles at the tableau. “The place looks beautiful, Alex.”

    Alex makes an airy, self-deprecating sound. “Well, you know,” she says. “You say you’re not going traditional and the next thing you know you’re trying on white dresses, and fussing over centerpieces, and your sister is hand-painting your ketubah.”

    Lena’s eyes fly over to Kara’s, surprised. “You paint?”

    Kara squirms a little, awkward, and Lena can’t tell if it’s modesty or genuine embarrassment that colors her cheeks. “Yeah,” she says. “I do, a little.” She hides behind her lashes, but Lena catches a peeved look from Alex.

    “‘A little’,” Alex mocks. “It’s a masterpiece. I’m not kidding about you selling them, like, professionally. You would make a killing.”

    Jess returns with a handful of tumblers. “Maybe Kara should have charged you for yours.”

    Kara purses her lips at her sister, looking cheeky. “Is it too late now?”

    Alex pours the scotch. “That depends,” she says. “Did you intend to pay me back for that expensive suit you’re wearing tomorrow?”

    Lena is — actually really looking forward to seeing Kara in said suit, but she also feels a little bad. “I didn’t know you were an artist,” she says. “You never mentioned it.”

    Kara blinks back up at her, her gaze like a physical touch, like careful fingertips plucking at resonant strings inside Lena’s chest.

    “There’s a lot you don’t know about me yet,” Kara promises, and then she adds, casually, taking her eyes away before the moment lingers between them too long again, “Alex also tried to make me ghost-write her wedding vows.”

    Alex snaps to wide-eyed fury like a switch has been flipped, shushing her sister frantically, looking around to see who else may have overheard. “That was supposed to be between you and me.”

    “Maybe if you’d paid me.” Kara winks.

    Alex settles back into the pillows, still a little ruffled. She grumbles, “All I asked for was some minor editing.”

    Kara gives her a hard look. “You can’t edit an empty page.”

    Alex sighs. “Don’t tell Sam.”

    “Her vows are a tearjerker, by the way,” Kara informs her. “Don’t say I didn’t warn you.”

    “She can jerk all she wants,” Alex quips. “I don’t cry.”

    Kara snorts. “Uh, not true. You cried through the entire series finale of The Good Place.”

    “Okay,” Alex allows with a dramatic roll of her eyes, “I cried at a piece of extremely well-crafted media one time.”

    “Right but it was, like, embarrassingly hard.”

    “That’s what she said.” Sam has sauntered out through the patio doors. She looks good, happy, healthy, but also like she could use a quick little four-hundred-year-long nap. “Hello people who are late,” she says, pressing a kiss to the top of Kara’s head before depositing herself into Lena's lap. “Does anyone happen to know what the cutoff age is for getting yourself emancipated from a parent?”

    “Pretty sure you’re there,” Lena says. “Misplaced loyalties notwithstanding.”

    Sam gives her a dark look. “Look who’s talking.”

    Another lanky, dark-haired figure appears in the doorway. It’s Ruby, looking ten feet taller than she did the last time Lena saw her. She’s in her pajamas, a thick down coat and new winter boots pulled on over them. She’s also wearing a knitted reindeer hat.

    “Baby,” Sam says, “what are you doing up? We’ve got a big day tomorrow.”

    “I know,” Ruby says, matter-of-fact. “That’s why I need to talk to Kara and Aunt Lena alone.” She eyes her mother meaningfully, some sort of serious, silent exchange transpiring between them, and Sam nods.

    “Go. Be quick about it.”

    They follow her to the chairs set up by the fireplace in the lobby, Ruby directing the two of them into a loveseat before sinking down in the armchair across from it. It occurs to Lena how much Ruby resembles her mother, in the moment — all business and professionalism when she wants to be.

    In spite of the outfit she’s wearing.

    Kara seems to pick up on the gravity of the moment, as well. She clears her throat. “You know, Rubes,” she says, “Starting tomorrow, you'll be required by law to call me ‘aunt’ Kara, too.”

    Ruby looks dubious. “Maybe. That’s kind of what brings us here.” She lets her gaze drift from Kara to Lena as she continues, “Considering you’ll be becoming my aunt, and you already are, I want to ask you both for your blessing.”

    “You’ve got it,” Kara says easily. “But also, what for?”

    Ruby takes a breath, and then announces, “Tomorrow, after the ceremony, I want to give Alex the papers she would need to sign in order to adopt me.”

    The volume of Kara’s overjoyed response almost throws Lena from their seat. Kara bridges the distance between herself and Ruby in a single leap, scooping her up in a tight hug.

    But Lena freezes. She asks, “Why is the hair on the back of my neck standing up?”

    Over Kara’s shoulder, Ruby’s eyes have gone wide with alarm.

    Lena turns.

    Alex is standing in the doorway to the restaurant, mimicking Ruby’s expression, holding her breath.

    “Shit,” Ruby says.

    “Language,” Alex whispers, moving only her lips to do so, and even those just barely.

    Kara lowers Ruby carefully back to the ground.

    “Mom!” Ruby says sternly when she’s landed, loud enough to wake the dead.

    Sam appears in the doorway next to Alex not a nanosecond later. “What’s going on?”

    Ruby pulls her hat off of her head and shoves it in the general direction of Kara’s hands. Her jaw is set with the same cool determination Lena has witnessed in Sam’s more formidable boardroom moments. She’s almost fifteen, Lena realizes. How do these things happen so fast?

    “Alexandra— almost Arias,” Ruby revises smoothly, “would you do me the honor of officially becoming my mom?”

    Alex’s entire body jerks with what appears to be an enormous effort to not start sobbing. She holds it together, staring at Ruby like the girl is made of miracles before rolling tearful eyes up at Sam. “I can’t believe this is the second time your daughter has proposed to me,” she croaks, “and you haven’t even done it once.”

    Sam gives her a glittering smile. “Our daughter,” she says.

    And then Alex genuinely begins to bawl.

    -

    They regroup around the fire outside, after Sam and Alex finally return Ruby to her bed, and they talk, and drink, and smoke — though Sam is sober tonight, not trusting herself not to overdo it with Patricia in such close proximity, and only her and Lena indulge in the occasional puff from the Habanos Sam passes around. Jess explains smoking makes her nauseous, and Alex turns down both the one she’s offered herself and the one Kara is about to accept with a very decisive, very motherly hand.

    “I can’t believe this is my last night as a single woman,” Sam says from where she’s now draped over Alex's lap. “I’m never going to fuck, like, anyone else, ever again.”

    Alex gently knocks her glass into Sam’s head, her eyes still glossy and faintly red. “You haven’t been single for years, you doof.”

    “Sure,” Sam says. “But by this time tomorrow I’ll be off the market for good.” She stretches out one of her unreasonably long legs to dig a sharp toe into Lena’s hip. “Kind of expected you to fight for me a little harder there, Luthor.”

    Lena is barely paying attention, captivated by the way Kara can’t seem to stop looking at her each time she brings the cigar to her lips. Kara is seated right across from her, staring, cheeks pink, as Lena releases delicate tendrils of blue smoke into the chilly evening air.

    “No thanks,” Lena says on the exhale. “You’re too high-maintenance. Alex can have you.” She puts her cigar down, careful not to knock off the ash, and adds, “I think I need someone who’s made of stronger stuff. You took one look at my issues and fled.”

    Kara is looking a little loopy at this point. She’s not blinking, fixated solely on the movement of Lena’s hand, unresponsive even when Lena says her name. It takes her sister’s pointed elbow to bring her back to planet earth.

    “I wasn't!” Kara blurts, apropos of nothing.

    Lena looks at her, quirking an eyebrow, and slowly strokes a fingertip along the length of her cigar. “Do you want to try?” she asks, her voice dipping dangerously close to temptation.

    The moment turns heady quick, too much so perhaps, considering their present company. But Lena’s defenses are down, and she’s not in a hurry to bring them back up, not with the way Kara is looking at her right now. She’s all dark wide-eyed hunger, and Lena can picture it perfectly — Kara kneeling down in front of her, blinking up as Lena brings the lipstick-marked side of her cigar to Kara’s waiting lips.

    But Sam, happily oblivious, half-drunk from the feeling of Alex’s fingers in her hair, interferes. She reaches back over her shoulder and says, “Here.”

    Kara glances down at Sam’s cigar as if she doesn’t understand what Sam is offering, but then it’s between her fingers, and Alex is warning her sister urgently not to inhale, and Kara explodes into a coughing fit anyway, and Lena is made to watch as the whole thing turns into a slapstick comedy instead of the seduction she’d imagined.

    Jess’ nose is twitching, her dark eyes flitting sharply between Sam and Lena. “I’m sorry,” she says, “but the image of you and Sam as lovers—”

    Lena sputters. “Well stop picturing it, god.”

    “We work way better as friends,” Sam tells her warmly.

    “It’s true,” Lena agrees. “You were an awful lover.”

    Sam squawks. “Fraud and defamation.”

    Lena takes one final draw before setting the cigar aside, allowing the rest of it to burn away. “No, I’m serious,” she says. “You should be concerned, Kara. Your sister really is getting a shitty deal.”

    Sam is still grumbling in Alex’s lap. “Slander,” she says. “Outrageous lies.”

    “Speaking of shitty deals,” Alex sighs. “Thanks to Sam’s mother, we’re short a room.”

    “Ugh,” Sam spits. “‘Why put me up at the cabin, Samantha?’” she mocks, her timbre rising in an uncanny approximation of her mother’s voice. “‘If you didn’t want me at your wedding, you could have spared me the trouble of traveling all this way and simply not have invited me.’”

    “Which we’ll admit was kind of the idea?” Alex points out. “But still, c’mon. It’s like, a ten minute drive.”

    Lena’s mind provides her with a quick but powerful reminder of the dreams she has been trying so very hard to forget. Her subconscious has been busy conjuring new ways of wanting Kara — her hand between Lena's shoulder blades as she rocks into her from behind, whispering depravities; Lena beneath her, urging Kara up her body so she can guide her down onto her mouth.

    The cabin had featured prominently in most of these fantasies.

    Lena blurts out, “Well, if Patricia doesn’t want to stay there—”

    Kara understands the implications, but it takes her a moment, which means her eager “We can share!” lags a couple of moments behind Jess protesting, “I am not staying in a cabin if the resort is an option.”

    “That works out, then,” Alex decides, relieved, already rising and pulling Sam up from the daybed. “Kara and Jess can take the two-bedroom suite, and Sam will drive Lena to the house.”

    “Oh, no,” Lena tries.

    But Sam, her best friend and most stalwart, most clueless tormenter, swiftly intercepts her protests. “You’ve all been drinking,” she points out. “It’s no trouble.”

    Kara stares after them, pouting, making confused-yet-urgent eye contact as Sam whisks Lena away. Lena sends her significant looks in return, feeling stupid and flustered, unsure how to fix the situation without outing them to all of their friends.

    Jess rolls her eyes at both of them, looking like she’s profoundly over it all.

    “Thank you for this,” Alex tells Lena as she drops her bags into the trunk. “We had the cleaners come in this week, the master bedroom is all made up for you. It has a 72” bath. You will love it.”

    Lena just keeps nodding, feeling like the bullfrog on the dashboard of Kara’s car.

    Bobblehead Lena Luthor.

    Maybe L-Corp should market them.

    -

    Lena doesn’t understand why her texts to Kara keep refusing to go through when she’d had zero trouble ordering DoorDash.

    She’s in the kitchen, setting up, her hair still wet from her bath. The formerly undisclosed floor heating in the living room is working with the fire in the hearth to bring the temperature in the cabin up to a cozy seventy-five degrees, and Lena is luxuriating in it, wearing only an oversized cable-knit sweater and a thick pair of socks.

    There’s a knock on the door, and there’s Kara — holding her bag in one hand, and her Waterpik® in the other.

    She huffs. “Your assistant is a menace.”

    Lena basks in it, for a moment. The pleasure of seeing Kara here, in the place where it all started, is something akin to pure relief. “I thought you’d be my food delivery,” she says.

    Kara perks up immediately. “You ordered takeout?”

    Lena smiles as she steps aside. “Something tells me I’ll be ordering more.”

    Kara closes the door behind her and sets her luggage down, fussing with it a moment, eyes flickering between Lena’s state of undress, her bare face, the water droplets still clinging to the ends of her hair. Kara sucks her bottom lip between her teeth, doesn’t even seem aware that she’s doing it.

    “I took advantage of that bath,” Lena explains. “Don’t judge. It took you seven and a half years to get here.”

    Kara is looking hungrier by the minute. “I don’t mean to intrude,” she says, looking back at her duffel bag, “or assume I could just—” She sighs. “I never get reception here,” she says. She holds up her phone. “I tried to ask.”

    Lena pushes herself up against Kara’s body, lifting up on the balls of her feet to make up for their height difference. Kara smells like the chilly winter air, a hint of smoke from the fire still clinging to her hair. Lena curls her fingers around the back of her neck and says, “Darling. I was hoping you’d come.”

    Kara's nose brushes softly against Lena's as she runs her thumbs along the bottom hem of Lena’s sweater.

    And then there’s another knock on the door.

    Kara manages to look both thrilled and disappointed at the contents of the bags. “These are groceries,” she observes. “Also did you just give that guy a five hundred dollar tip?”

    “You have a good eye,” Lena teases. “I can’t believe I never figured out you were an artist as well as a wordsmith.”

    “There aren’t any vegetables in here.” Kara looks elated at this discovery. “What are you making?”

    They’ve drifted back into each other’s space. “I felt bad about you missing dessert,” Lena tells Kara’s mouth.

    “And when you say dessert,” Kara says, her tongue licking out at her bottom lip.

    “I mean the kind you eat.” At Kara’s dark look, Lena clarifies, “With a spoon.” She twists out of Kara’s tentative grasp, raising an eyebrow at her answering pout. “Do you still remember how to separate an egg?”

    Kara slides her hands into her back pockets as if she's trying to keep herself from reaching for Lena again. “And, so, would that be the only thing on the menu, tonight?" she asks. "Or…”

    “We’ll see,” Lena hums, her cheeks warming a little at Kara's intensity. “One step at a time, right?”

    The corners of Kara’s mouth curve up, sharp. “We’d better get to work, then.” Her eyes are still drifting, lingering at the hem of Lena’s sweater.

    Lena smirks at her. “Are you going to ask me to put clothes on again?”

    Kara scoffs at the suggestion. “Not a chance.”

    -

    They make two-hour tiramisu, because it’s almost midnight and Lena isn’t that imaginative, and she hadn’t been able to stop thinking about licking cream off of Kara’s fingers the entire time she was sitting in Sam’s car.

    Kara is distracted, too; and not only by the proximity of the savoiardi cookies she isn’t allowed to eat, or the old fashioned Lena gives her, or her view of Lena’s legs. She’s moved her bag to a chair by the kitchen table, and she keeps looking over at it as if she’s afraid it will try to escape.

    They play cards while their dessert is setting in the fridge. Kara wins three hands in a row — fortuna favet fatuis, as Lex used to sneer — and says, the cocky rumble in her voice sending a rush of goosebumps down Lena's back, “If I’d known my luck was going to be this good, I would have suggested we make things a little more interesting.”

    Lena stops shuffling, lifting an eyebrow in response. “I’m not taking any more of my clothes off," she bluffs.

    Kara’s eyes flit to her duffel bag again. And then she suggests, “Maybe we can trade secrets, instead.”

    “Sure.” Lena considers for a brief moment, setting the cards aside. “I sleep with an occlusal splint.”

    Kara just stares at her, vaguely startled and comically confused.

    “You know, a night guard?” Lena bares her teeth and waves a pinkie finger at them. “Dentist was worried I'd grind these down to nubs.”

    Kara blanches. “What?”

    “What, ‘What?’” Lena winks, grinning. “We’re exchanging secrets, right?”

    “Right.” Kara blinks. “Yes.”

    Lena leans forward on her elbows and crosses her arms, feeling gratified when Kara tracks the movement up to the wide cowl neck of her sweater, the way it gaps in front to reveal the skin beneath. Lena says, her voice lilting low, “Not the kind of secret you were hoping for, maybe.”

    When Kara looks up, her gaze is like a brand. “Maybe not.”

    Lena reaches across the table, where Kara’s hand is gripped tight around her glass. She slots her fingers between Kara's knuckles, a featherlight caress. Kara swallows.

    “What’s in the bag?” Lena asks. And then she watches, intrigued, as Kara’s ears turn a furious pink.

    Kara looks wounded. “Two jobs,” she points out, pouting.

    Kara’s pout turns plaintive. “I’m the one who makes the hora happen!” she cries.

    Alex sighs, rubbing her brow, furiously winding her finger in the air. “And…?”

    Kara’s hurt expression fades as realization dawns. “I keep Patty off the stage,” she whispers.

    Through the open bathroom door, they hear the faint but unmistakable sounds of someone belting out the climax to ‘Memory’.

    “Oh,” Kara hushes, mortified. “Patricia, no.”

    “Patricia yes,” Alex grits out.

    Lena tugs on Alex’s sleeve as she hurries them into the hallway, making sure to look appropriately penitent. “Would you mind not telling Sam about this little encounter?” she asks her. “I know you’re married now, but. She gets a little—”

    “—much?” Alex scoffs. “You think I don’t know that? Why do you think I never came clean about losing that stupid bet?”

    Lena’s fingers fly to her nape when Alex gestures at it, frowning. Apparently Kara’s efforts were more successful than she thought. Lena obediently slides the buttons back into place as she says, “I always figured that was your pride getting in the way.”

    Sam and Jess are in a seating area just outside the ballroom. Jess bounces upright when she spots them, smiling far too knowingly. Sam stays where she is, collapsed in her chair with her skirts draped around her, looking like a deeply traumatized merengue.

    “There you are,” Jess says, sliding her hand around Kara’s bicep. “You know it’s bad manners to leave your date alone at a party, right?”

    “So we’re dating, huh?” Kara winks down at her. “Does that mean you’re going to buy me a drink?”

    Jess rolls her eyes affectionately. “It’s an open bar.”

    Kara puts her hand on Jess' where it's still curled around Kara’s arm, patting it gently as they walk away. “I’ll take that as a yes.”

    Lena and Alex grab Sam, hoisting her up by her elbows in a slightly-less-dramatic approximation of a crutch carry. “How are you doing, honey?” Lena asks as the three of them make their way back to their table arm in arm.

    Sam takes a resolute breath. “I’m actually awesome,” she decides. “Watching my mother use my wedding as a platform to try and revive the sad remains of her career has dealt me extensive emotional damage, but I am also, just, profoundly, no— ecstatically— married.”

    Lena huffs out a laugh. “It really is a beautiful wedding, guys.”

    Alex smiles. Sam beams. “I know,” she says. “Thank you so much for picking up the tab.”

    Lena halts, stopping all three of them in their tracks. “Pardon me?”

    “Well.” Sam shrugs. “You do owe me twenty grand.”

    Lena sputters. “You gave that money to Jess, not me,” she says. “We have got to stop drinking with that woman.”

    Alex’s head whirls around at her so fast Lena fears she’s given herself whiplash.

    Sam hums. “That's what my wife says.”

    Alex is staring at them. “The bet from last year? About these two absolute—” She’s pale now, gasping. “You gave Jess twenty grand?”

    Sam slips out of Lena’s hold, sliding a loving hand around Alex’s waist. “We’re married now, babe,” she says, “so technically we gave Jess twenty grand.”

    Then she taps her heel to Lena’s, nodding to where Kara and Jess are standing together at the bar. Kara is leaning in, smiling, as Jess presses a napkin to her mouth. Sam says, “I think your assistant is trying to steal your girl.”

    Lena sighs. “Stop calling her that.”

    Sam glints her teeth at Lena’s response, her eyes sparkling dangerously as she slings an arm around her wife’s shoulders. “Is there a different term you would like us to use?”

    Lena rolls her eyes up at her. “Jess,” she says pointedly, “is our friend, not my assistant. Also I’m thinking a promotion may be in order. Maybe we can make her a partner.” She looks at Kara, who has turned away from Jess and is now searching the room, looking almost painfully handsome in her bespoke suit. When their eyes meet across the crowded room, Kara's face lights up like sunshine, and Lena feels that familiar wave of affection rise up in her chest again.

    Alex says, “Maybe it’s time we call all things by their proper names, Luthor.”

    Sam blows her wife a tender kiss. “Brava,” she says. “Encore.”

    Patricia is seated at a table at least twenty feet away, but her head still ticks up sharply at Sam’s words.

    “Hey,” Lena says, bumping her shoulder softly into Sam’s and catching Alex's eyes. “You know I love you both, right? You and that implausibly well-adjusted daughter of yours.”

    The look of endearment they give her is one Lena is very accustomed to seeing on Sam, but it’s an entirely new phenomenon to see it mirrored on Alex’s face. For a moment Lena almost thinks Alex might hug her, but her wife beats her to it. “Go tell Kara that, you sap,” Sam says, pressing a kiss to the side of Lena’s head. “I’m proud of you.”

    When Lena reaches Kara and Jess, Ruby has found them too. Lena worries about her for a moment, eyeing the glass of champagne in her hand, but Ruby hands it over immediately with a hurried “I was just smelling it, I swear.”

    Jess’ hand is smoothing down one of Kara’s lapels, but she removes it when Lena joins them. She says, with emphasis, “Ms. Luthor.”

    Lena side eyes her, but ignores it, catching the teasing glint in Jess’ smirk. “Jess,” she says. “Hitting on Kara, again?”

    Ruby giggles. Kara flushes pink, lips already parting in protest. But Jess says, “Obviously.”

    Lena steps close into Kara’s other side, sliding her hand up Kara’s shoulder and into the soft hair at the back of her neck. She watches Kara’s eyelids flutter against her cheeks before pressing a kiss to the pink skin just below. “I can’t really blame you,” she tells Jess. “This one’s a keeper.”

    Kara is watching her closely as she pulls away, looking— hopeful?

    Lena’s lipstick has left a faint mark on her cheek.

    “Come on, Ruby,” Jess says, grabbing the girl’s hand and tugging her away. “Let’s leave these two lovebirds alone.”

    Lena watches them make their way to the dancefloor, where they take turns twirling for a few moments before an impromptu floss dance lesson ensues. Sam and Alex are over by the wedding cake with Eliza — who is exactly as tremendously lovely as Kara described, by the way — gushing over the framed ketubah displayed there. Kara has embellished it with a somehow delicate explosion of color, an intricate design featuring the blue thistles and red ranunculi Sam and Alex had picked for their bouquets. The brides are laughing, leaned together close, and radiant.

    Lena takes Kara’s hand and pulls her into the swaying crowd.

    Kara is surprised. It doesn’t delay her hands though; they’ve already slipped around Lena’s waist. She says, “I thought you didn’t dance.”

    Lena rests her arms on Kara’s shoulders. “My therapist says I need to expand my comfort zone.”

    Kara’s face is as radiant as her sister’s. Even the Colorado midwinter sun has been enough to bring out her freckles. Lena moves a hand to Kara’s face, but instead of swiping her thumb over the mark her lips have left like she’d intended, she finds herself gently removing Kara’s glasses.

    It shouldn’t feel so significant, this simple gesture, sliding them down Kara’s wrinkling nose and tucking them into the pocket of her suit jacket, but it reverberates inside her like the crack and shift of continental plates. Kissing her, after that, in public, not caring who can see, is like nothing at all. Kara lets out a sweetly startled sigh when Lena licks into it a little, running the tip of her tongue over the seam of Kara’s lips.

    “Mm.” Lena pulls away, bringing her fingers to her own mouth, puzzled. She furrows her brow and asks, “Why do you taste like pumpkin pie?”

    Kara’s features brighten impossibly more. The small specks of gold around her pupils sparkle and flash. “It’s flavored chapstick,” she explains. “It’s seasonal. I have it in sugar cookie and candy cane, as well.” She rummages in her jacket pockets, digging up three little festive-looking tubes. “Do you want to try?”

    Lena looks down at them, and then she looks at Kara, and Lena loves her. She loves her. It’s unstoppable. It can’t be helped.

    So she kisses her again, pulls her in by her dumb hair and kisses her until not a hint of pumpkin spice remains, until Kara is looking at her, breathless, and a little dazed.

    Lena slants her lashes up at her. “Well?” she says. “I’m ready. What are you waiting for?”

    Kara sways toward her slightly, giving her one of those squinty smiles that always make Lena feel uncomfortably squishy. There’s a promise in that smile. And Lena doesn't think it's too soon to say it, anymore. They’re here together, where it all started, surrounded by their friends, drinking another one of Sam’s excellent wines.

    “Go on,” Lena says, tugging gently on Kara’s tie. “Put on another one. I want to try the sugar cookie, next.”




    Chapter End Notes

    Here we are.

    This story would not be what it has grown into without your support. Thank you all, so, so much. It’s been one hell of a couple of years, hasn’t it? I’m so glad we got to survive them together.
